inQUESTION: Which two countries were recently removed from the FATF grey list after an Anti-Money Laundering and Combating the Financing of Terrorism (AML/CFT) compliance review?
A. Algeria and Angola
B. Namibia and Kenya
C. Algeria and Namibia
D. Namibia and South Africa
E. Algeria and Kenya
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – Algeria and Namibia</b>

Both countries were removed from the FATF grey list after addressing their strategic AML/CFT deficiencies. With this, the grey list now stands at <mark>22 jurisdictions</mark>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Grey list = <b>Jurisdictions Under Increased Monitoring</b> – countries working with FATF to strengthen their AML/CFT regimes.</li><li>Black list = <b>High-Risk Jurisdictions subject to a Call for Action</b> – <b>North Korea (DPRK), Iran and Myanmar</b> remain on it.</li><li><b>FATF</b> develops and promotes policies against money laundering, terrorist financing and financing of weapons of mass destruction.</li></ul>
TAG: FATF, International news/Oneliner

QUESTION: Which body approved the construction of India's first underwater twin-tube road-cum-rail tunnel beneath the Brahmaputra River in Assam?
A. Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA)
B. NITI Aayog
C. Ministry of Road Transport and Highways
D. Railway Board
E. National Highways Authority of India
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA)</b>

The CCEA approved this landmark project aimed at transforming connectivity in Northeast India.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Total capital cost:</b> <mark>Rs.18,662.02 crore</mark>.</li><li><b>Construction technology:</b> Tunnel Boring Machine (TBM).</li><li><b>Implementation model:</b> Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC).</li><li><b>Road configuration:</b> four-lane access-controlled greenfield corridor; two lanes in each tube; railway in one of the tubes.</li><li>Assam – Capital: <b>Dispur</b>; CM: <b>Himanta Biswa Sarma</b>; Governor: <b>Lakshman Prasad Acharya</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Infrastructure, National news/Oneliner

QUESTION: Under the amended anti-paper leak framework, the maximum financial penalty for individual offenders has been raised from Rs.10 lakh to:
A. Rs.25 lakh
B. Rs.1 crore
C. Rs.5 crore
D. Rs.50 lakh
E. Rs.10 crore
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Rs.50 lakh</b>

For individual actors leaking papers or manipulating results, the maximum fine jumps from <b>Rs.10 lakh to Rs.50 lakh</b>, and the minimum prison term rises from 3 to 5 years (up to 10 years).

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The Union Cabinet approved amendments to the <b>Public Examinations (Prevention of Unfair Means) Act, 2024</b>; <b>Lok Sabha passed</b> the Bill on <mark>29 July 2026</mark>.</li><li><b>Service providers:</b> maximum fine raised from Rs.1 crore to Rs.5 crore; debarment from 4 to 8 years.</li></ul>

QUESTION: Which ministry granted Miniratna Category-I status to MECON Limited?
A. Ministry of Heavy Industries
B. Ministry of Steel
C. Ministry of Mines
D. Ministry of Coal
E. Ministry of Power
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Ministry of Steel</b>

MECON is a <b>Schedule 'A' Central Public Sector Enterprise (CPSE)</b> and now holds Miniratna Category-I status.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Eligibility (Cat-I):</b> profit for 3 consecutive years; pre-tax profit of <mark>Rs.30 crore or more</mark> in at least one of the previous three years; positive net worth.</li><li><b>Benefit:</b> the Board can approve capital expenditure up to <b>Rs.500 crore or the company's net worth, whichever is lower</b>, without prior Government approval.</li></ul>

QUESTION: Which product was the first to receive a Geographical Indication (GI) tag in India?
A. Kancheepuram Silk
B. Pochampally Ikat
C. Basmati Rice
D. Mysore Silk
E. Darjeeling Tea
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E – Darjeeling Tea</b>

Darjeeling Tea from <b>West Bengal</b> was India's first GI-tagged product (agricultural category), registered in <mark>2004-05</mark>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>A GI is a sign used for products from a specific geographical region with a special quality, reputation or characteristic linked to the place of origin.</li><li><b>GI Registry:</b> Chennai, Tamil Nadu; administered by the <b>Ministry of Commerce and Industry</b>.</li><li>A GI registration is valid for <b>10 years</b>.</li><li>West Bengal received <b>23 new GI tags</b> (total 59) – the 3rd highest among states.</li></ul>
TAG: GI Tag, National news/Oneliner

B. Delhi – Panipat
C. Ambala – Chandigarh

E. Hisar – Bhiwani
ANSWER: D

QUESTION: Which of the following is a condition for a CPSE to get Miniratna Category-I status?
A. Pre-tax profit of Rs.100 crore in each of the last three years
B. Net worth of more than Rs.15,000 crore
C. Average annual turnover of more than Rs.25,000 crore
D. At least 3 'Excellent' MoUs in the last five years
E. Profit for 3 consecutive years and pre-tax profit of Rs.30 crore or more in at least one of the previous three years
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E</b>

Miniratna Category-I requires <b>profit for 3 consecutive years</b>, <mark>pre-tax profit of Rs.30 crore or more</mark> in at least one of the previous three years, and <b>positive net worth</b>.

<b>🧠 Why not the others?</b>
<ul><li><b>A</b> – not a Miniratna condition.</li><li><b>B and C</b> – these are <b>Maharatna</b> criteria (net worth above Rs.15,000 crore; turnover above Rs.25,000 crore).</li><li><b>D</b> – this is a <b>Navratna</b> criterion.</li></ul>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Category-II:</b> profits continuously for the last three years and positive net worth (8 companies); <b>Category-I:</b> 50 companies.</li></ul>
TAG: CPSE, Economy/Statement Based

QUESTION: Up to what limit can the Board of a Miniratna Category-I CPSE approve capital expenditure without prior Government approval?
A. Rs.250 crore or net worth, whichever is lower
B. Rs.500 crore or net worth, whichever is lower
C. Rs.500 crore or net worth, whichever is higher
D. Rs.1,000 crore or net worth, whichever is lower
E. Rs.1,000 crore or 50% of net worth
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Rs.500 crore or net worth, whichever is lower</b>

This financial autonomy is the <b>major benefit</b> of Miniratna Category-I status, recently granted to <b>MECON Limited</b> by the Ministry of Steel.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>MECON is a <b>Schedule 'A' CPSE</b>.</li><li>CPSE hierarchy: <b>14 Maharatnas, 29 Navratnas, 58 Miniratnas</b> (Category-I: 50; Category-II: 8).</li></ul>

A. 40
B. 50
C. 55
D. 60
E. 75
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – 60</b>

<b>📌 Criteria for Navratna status</b>
<ul><li>Miniratna Category-I status along with a <b>Schedule 'A'</b> listing.</li><li>At least <b>3 'Excellent' or 'Very Good' MoUs</b> in the last five years.</li><li>Composite score of <mark>60 or above out of 100</mark> in six selected performance parameters.</li></ul>
<b>⚡ Memory trick:</b> <mark>Miniratna-I → Navratna → Maharatna.</mark>

QUESTION: NaMo Green Rail, India's first indigenous hydrogen-powered train, uses which technology to generate electricity from hydrogen?
A. Solid Oxide Fuel Cell
B. Alkaline Fuel Cell
C. Lithium-ion Battery
D. Proton Exchange Membrane (PEM) Fuel Cell
E. Phosphoric Acid Fuel Cell
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Proton Exchange Membrane (PEM) Fuel Cell</b>

Hydrogen stored in high-pressure cylinders reacts with oxygen to generate electricity.

The roadmap is a strategic framework to transform maternal and newborn healthcare and <mark>aligns India's health policies with Sustainable Development Goal 3</mark>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Launched by:</b> Union Health Minister Jagat Prakash Nadda.</li><li><b>Vision:</b> reduce the Maternal Mortality Ratio (MMR) to below <b>70 per 1,00,000 live births by 2030</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: SUMAN, Government schemes/Full Form

QUESTION: Under the revamped RBI Integrated Ombudsman Scheme effective 1 July 2026, a complaint can be escalated to the RBI Ombudsman within how many days if the regulated entity fails to respond or the customer is dissatisfied?
A. 30 days
B. 60 days
C. 90 days
D. 120 days
E. 1 year
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – 90 days</b>

Under RB-IOS 2026, a customer first complains to the regulated entity. If there is no reply within <b>30 days</b> (or any longer RBI/NPCI/card-network timeline) or the customer is unhappy, the complaint can be escalated to the Ombudsman.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Time limit to approach the Ombudsman was cut from <mark>1 year to 90 days</mark>.</li><li>The scheme continues the idea of <b>"One Nation, One Ombudsman"</b>.</li><li>Filing and resolving a complaint is <b>completely free of charge</b>.</li></ul>

QUESTION: As per the Banking Laws Amendment, up to how many nominees can be named for a single bank account in India?
A. Four
B. Two
C. Five
D. Six
E. Three
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Four</b>

Nominees can be named under two methods – <b>simultaneous</b> and <b>successive</b> nomination.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Simultaneous:</b> concurrent rights; percentage share for each must total exactly <b>100%</b> (e.g., Spouse 50%, Child A 25%, Child B 25%).</li><li><b>Successive:</b> nominees in ranked sequence; the next becomes active only if the previous one dies or is unavailable.</li></ul>

QUESTION: Basel I, introduced in 1988, mainly focused on ______ risk and required banks to maintain a minimum capital adequacy ratio of:
A. credit; 9%
B. operational; 8%
C. market; 8%
D. credit; 8%
E. credit; 10.5%
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – credit; 8%</b>

Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR/CRAR) = <b>Capital ÷ Risk-Weighted Assets × 100</b>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Example: RWA of ₹100 crore → minimum capital of <b>₹8 crore</b> under Basel I.</li><li>In India CAR is called <b>CRAR – Capital to Risk-Weighted Assets Ratio</b>.</li><li>Basel Committee was established in <b>1974</b> (Basel city, Switzerland).</li></ul>

QUESTION: Which parameter of the RBI's Financial Inclusion Index carries the highest weightage, and what is its weight?

B. Usage – 35%
C. Quality – 35%
D. Usage – 45%
E. Access – 20%
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Usage – 45%</b>

The FI Index has three broad parameters with weights that add up to 100%.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Access – 35%</b>: how easily financial services are available.</li><li><b>Usage – 45%</b>: how frequently and effectively services are used.</li><li><b>Quality – 20%</b>: financial literacy, consumer protection, inequalities and deficiencies in service.</li></ul>

<b>⚡ Memory trick:</b> <mark>35 – 45 – 20 = Access – Usage – Quality.</mark>

QUESTION: In a hypothecation arrangement, the movable asset offered as security to the lender:
A. is an immovable property
B. is handed over to a third-party custodian
C. is immediately owned by the bank
D. is deposited with the bank
E. remains in the possession of the borrower
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E – remains in the possession of the borrower</b>

Hypothecation: security over a <b>movable asset</b> where the borrower keeps possession (e.g., stock, vehicles).

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Collateral</b> – security/assurance given against a loan (common term).</li><li><b>Mortgage</b> – immovable property (land/house) given as security.</li><li><b>Pledge</b> – movable asset deposited with the bank to obtain a loan.</li></ul>

<b>⚡ Memory trick:</b> <mark>Mortgage = land/house · Pledge = goods go to bank · Hypothecation = goods stay with you.</mark>
TAG: Non-Performing Assets, Banking news/Banking Terminology
